    2744             : 
    2745             : /* "Improved Collision detection and Response" */
    2746           0 : static bool getLowestRoot(
    2747             :     const float a, const float b, const float c, const float maxR, float *root)
    2748             : {
    2749             :   /* Check if a solution exists */
    2750           0 :   const float determinant = b * b - 4.0f * a * c;
    2751             : 
    2752             :   /* If determinant is negative it means no solutions. */
    2753           0 :   if (determinant >= 0.0f) {
    2754             :     /* calculate the two roots: (if determinant == 0 then
    2755             :      * x1==x2 but lets disregard that slight optimization) */
    2756           0 :     const float sqrtD = sqrtf(determinant);
    2757           0 :     float r1 = (-b - sqrtD) / (2.0f * a);
    2758           0 :     float r2 = (-b + sqrtD) / (2.0f * a);
    2759             : 
    2760             :     /* Sort so x1 <= x2 */
    2761           0 :     if (r1 > r2) {
    2762           0 :       std::swap(r1, r2);
    2763             :     }
    2764             : 
    2765             :     /* Get lowest root: */
    2766           0 :     if (r1 > 0.0f && r1 < maxR) {
    2767           0 :       *root = r1;
    2768           0 :       return true;
    2769             :     }
    2770             : 
    2771             :     /* It is possible that we want x2 - this can happen */
    2772             :     /* if x1 < 0 */
    2773           0 :     if (r2 > 0.0f && r2 < maxR) {
    2774           0 :       *root = r2;
    2775           0 :       return true;
    2776             :     }
    2777             :   }
    2778             :   /* No (valid) solutions */
    2779           0 :   return false;
    2780             : }

    3500     2583537 : static bool point_in_slice(const float p[3],
    3501             :                            const float v1[3],
    3502             :                            const float l1[3],
    3503             :                            const float l2[3])
    3504             : {
    3505             :   /* What is a slice?
    3506             :    * Some math:
    3507             :    * a line including (l1, l2) and a point not on the line
    3508             :    * define a subset of R3 delimited by planes parallel to the line and orthogonal
    3509             :    * to the (point --> line) distance vector, one plane on the line one on the point,
    3510             :    * the room inside usually is rather small compared to R3 though still infinite
    3511             :    * useful for restricting (speeding up) searches
    3512             :    * e.g. all points of triangular prism are within the intersection of 3 "slices"

    5123           0 : static float snap_coordinate(float u)
    5124             : {
    5125             :   /* Adjust a coordinate value `u` to obtain a value inside the (closed) unit interval.
    5126             :    *   i.e. 0.0 <= snap_coordinate(u) <= 1.0.
    5127             :    * Due to round-off errors, it is possible that the value of `u` may be close to the boundary of
    5128             :    * the unit interval, but not exactly on it. In order to handle these cases, `snap_coordinate`
    5129             :    * checks whether `u` is within `epsilon` of the boundary, and if so, it snaps the return value
    5130             :    * to the boundary. */
    5131           0 :   if (u < 0.0f) {
    5132           0 :     u += 1.0f; /* Get back into the unit interval. */
    5133             :   }
    5134           0 :   BLI_assert(!(0.0f > u));
    5135           0 :   BLI_assert(!(1.0f < u));
    5136           0 :   const float epsilon = 0.25f / 65536.0f; /* i.e. Quarter of a texel on a 65536 x 65536 texture. */
    5137           0 :   if (u < epsilon) {
    5138           0 :     return 0.0f; /* `u` is close to 0, just return 0. */
    5139             :   }
    5140           0 :   if (1.0f - epsilon < u) {
    5141           0 :     return 1.0f; /* `u` is close to 1, just return 1. */
    5142             :   }
    5143           0 :   return u;
    5144             : }

    5732             :        * distance is being computed from. See figure 9 in the paper for the derivation. */
